# Family — decisions

One line per decision, appended the moment it is taken, never edited and never deleted. If a
decision is reversed, that reversal is a new row; the old one stays, because the record of
having thought otherwise is the useful part.

This file is authoritative on **project state**. `HANDOFF.md` §4 and the specification §11
used to both track it, neither outranked the other, and they drifted in three places — which
is why this file exists and why they now point here instead of keeping their own lists.

Everything above the rule below was back-filled on 2026-08-18 from those two lists. Dates are
the dates the decisions were actually taken; the back-fill is the only thing that happened
later.

| Date | Decision | Why, in one line | Where it landed |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-08 | No import from any other addon, unconditionally | Everything Family holds, it holds because it watched it happen | spec §9 |
| 2026-08-08 | Licence is GPL-3.0-or-later | Answers the question Family was created by: if the author stops, it stays open | `LICENSE`, every `.toc` |
| 2026-08-08 | Generated data is never committed, only regenerated | The generator and the client's own tables rebuild it whenever it is wanted | `.gitignore`, `tools/` |
| 2026-08-08 | Two addons, not a family of modules | Family has exactly one client — itself | `addons/Family/`, `addons/Family_UI/` |
| 2026-08-08 | Deploy script written from scratch | It mirrors Family's two folders and refuses to point anywhere else | `tools/Deploy.bat` |
| 2026-08-08 | History is never squashed; public at first release | The history is the record of how Family was built | policy |
| 2026-08-08 | Presentation pass deferred to one deliberate sweep | Panels are built for correctness first, polished once | `HANDOFF.md` §4.5 |
| 2026-08-09 | No item library, and no catalogue of where items come from | Family reports what members have and know; it does not advise | spec §2.5 |
| 2026-08-12 | Wide Family ships switched off until a real server has seen it | Nothing in it had crossed a live wire | spec §6.0 |
| 2026-08-12 | The logo is generated | `tools/GenerateIcon.py` draws it | `addons/Family_UI/Textures/Family.tga` |

---

| Date | Decision | Why, in one line | Where it landed |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-18 | `docs/DECISIONS.md` is the single record of project state | Two lists tracked it, neither outranked the other, and they disagreed in three places | this file |
| 2026-08-26 | A lesson is promoted once a check exists that catches it | A bite count measures luck; a check is what actually enforces the lesson | `LESSONS.md` preamble |
| 2026-08-26 | `sanitised` is a transformation of `workshop`, never a source | New content originating on the derived branch means the two have forked and the rebase has stopped being a transformation | policy |
| 2026-08-26 | The sanitisation rewrites `LESSONS.md` too, not only the three big documents | L-007 names a client API that collides with the removal list, so the public copy has to make the same point without the name | `LESSONS.md` on `sanitised` |
| 2026-08-26 | The public repository is seeded by an orphan commit, not by pushing `sanitised` | Sanitising a tree does not sanitise its history: every removed document would be one `git log -p` away | `git checkout --orphan`, at publication |
| 2026-08-26 | `uga/Family` is the working repository; the workshop repository is retired | Publication was the point at which the two stopped needing to be separate, and one repository cannot drift from itself | this clone's `origin` |
| 2026-08-26 | The `workshop` → sanitised transformation ends with the seed, superseding the three rows above it | With one working repository there is no derived branch to keep in step; those rows record how publication happened, not how work continues | policy |
| 2026-08-26 | The CurseForge key is removed from the retired repository | Both repositories carried `release.yml` and the key, so a stray `v*` tag on the workshop side would have published a zip built from the unsanitised history | repository secrets |
| 2026-08-26 | `major.minor.patch` means: major for a revamp or a new function, minor for fixes including a Blizzard patch that breaks something, patch for a `## Interface` bump alone | `RELEASING.md` said the numbers had to parse but never said what the three of them mean | `RELEASING.md` §version numbers |
| 2026-08-26 | The first release is `1.0.0-beta.1`, and `1.0` commits Family to migrating the saved variables rather than resetting them | `RELEASING.md` had held `1.0` back for exactly this promise; the schema number and its migrations are in place to keep it | `RELEASING.md` §version numbers, `Database.lua` |
| 2026-08-26 | The release job asks for `contents: write` itself, rather than the repository default being raised | Every other workflow stays read-only, and the reason sits in the file that needs it | `.github/workflows/release.yml` |
| 2026-08-26 | A GitHub release the workflow failed to create is made by hand, never by re-running the workflow | The CurseForge upload had already succeeded, and a re-run would have put a second file on the project | `L-009` |
| 2026-08-26 | The CurseForge project description is kept in the repository, not only in the web form | A page the project can be rejected over should be reviewable and diffable like everything else, and the next version should start from what the last one said | `docs/CURSEFORGE.md`, `RELEASING.md` step 1 |
| 2026-08-27 | The retired workshop checkout is renamed `dev/Family-history`, and `Deploy.bat`'s share points at `dev/Family-public` | Both trees hold a complete `addons/`, so a stale share path would have deployed the retired one silently rather than refusing | `tools/Deploy.bat` line 36 |
| 2026-08-27 | `Deploy.bat` warns, rather than refuses, when the source has no `Libs` | Deploying without the libraries is how the no-library path gets tested; deploying without noticing is how three clients lost theirs | `tools/Deploy.bat`, `L-010` |
| 2026-08-27 | The live check gates releases by tier: three clients recorded for a full release, one row for an alpha or a beta | `SMOKE.md` claimed `RELEASING.md` treated a missing row as a stop, and nothing in the tree had ever heard of the file | `docs/SMOKE.md`, `RELEASING.md` §every time, `tools/release.sh` |
| 2026-08-27 | A whole-family search draws nothing until it is asked, and the caption over its box has to say so | An empty panel under a caption describing the panel's other mode was read as the panel having broken | `Contents.lua`, `Professions.lua` |
| 2026-08-27 | The sides live in `Window.lua`, and every screen that groups by them groups the same three the same way | The summary's copy was the only one, and the gear grid needed the same order and the same two colours | `Window.lua` `UI.SIDE_*`, `Summary.lua`, `Character.lua` |
| 2026-08-27 | The whole family's gear is grouped by side, with a heading each, wherever there are two to tell apart | A row there is nineteen pictures and says nothing else about whose it is; the side was reachable only by hovering | `Character.lua`, `L-011` |
| 2026-08-27 | A borrowed member's *Last seen* reports when they were last shared, marked *shared*, rather than a dash | When they last played does not cross the wire, but when their family last told us does, and the panel already knew it | `Summary.lua` `CELL.seen`, `L-012`-style guard in the harness |
| 2026-08-27 | Wide Family stays off by default now that the live pass has reached it: the reason changes from untested to unasked-for | The 2026-08-12 condition is met, but shipping a sharing feature switched on is a separate decision and not one the pass takes | `MANUAL.md` §11, `About.lua`, `Slash.lua`, `CURSEFORGE.md` |
| 2026-08-27 | The beta.2 pass closes spec §11.0 and `HANDOFF` §4.6 and §4.7; §11.1 stays open and `SMOKE.md` gains the line that can close it | The checklist claimed a completed pass closed all four and had no line testing realm reach, so it closed the three it actually asked about | `Project high level specs.md` §6.0 and §11, `HANDOFF.md` 6 and 7, `SMOKE.md` |
| 2026-08-28 | `tools/Deploy.bat` in the repository is a template with placeholder paths; the real ones live only on the machine that runs it | It carried a LAN address and two install paths into a public repository from the seed commit, and the copy that is run has always been a separate one on the games PC anyway | `tools/Deploy.bat`, harness check *Deploy.bat names no real host* |
| 2026-08-28 | Family is translated into German, French, Spanish and Russian, and the English sentence is the lookup key | The store page had claimed five interface languages since before there was a string table; a key vocabulary of invented names is a second thing to keep in step, and a missing translation should degrade to readable English rather than to a key | `addons/Family/Locale.lua`, `addons/Family/Locales/`, `L-013` |
| 2026-08-28 | Game vocabulary comes from the client's own globals, never from a translation of ours, whatever its length | Blizzard has already decided what a thing is called in German; a shorter word of ours next to the game's own term for it reads as a fault, and a term is not ours to abbreviate | `Family:GameWord`, `Guild.lua`, `Contents.lua`, `Talents.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | A column too narrow for its heading is widened, and the room is taken from whatever has slack; a column is never put below its own heading | English is the shortest of the five languages and every fixed width in the tree was chosen by looking at it | `UI:FitColumns` in `Window.lua`, `Summary.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | Counted things are whole clauses chosen by count, never a stem with an `s` hung on the end | Fifteen sites formed their plural by appending a letter; Russian has three plural forms and none of them is that | `Summary.lua`, `Wide.lua`, `Character.lua`, `Talents.lua`, `Contents.lua`, `Quests.lua`, `Broker.lua`, `Guild.lua`, `Slash.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | `Family:Debug` stays English; `Family:Print` is translated | Narration is off by default and is read by whoever is diagnosing a fault, which is a different reader from the one every other string is written for | `Core.lua`, `Slash.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | The broker tooltip groups by realm and then by side, splitting only where two sides are actually known | Two members on one realm and opposite sides share no bank, no mailbox and no auction house, and one flat list reads as though they could pass things between them | `Broker.lua`, `Summary.lua` for the shape |
| 2026-08-28 | Buttons size themselves to their own label, and a row is bounded by the room it has | Every button width was chosen by looking at an English label; "Compétence requise" does not fit the 110 pixels "Skill needed" fits, and a row free to grow pushes whatever is beside it off the panel | `UI:LayOutRow`, `UI:FitButton`, `L-014` |
| 2026-08-28 | A row that still cannot fit says so in chat rather than drawing past the panel edge | Squeezing stops at the labels themselves, so overflow is possible and the only bad version of it is the silent one | `UI:LayOutRow`, mirroring the summary's column warning |
| 2026-08-28 | The summary reserves the height its footer and note actually need, measured after they are written | 28 pixels was enough for one line each in English and French wraps both to two, which drew the table's last row underneath them | `Summary.lua`, `GetStringHeight` |
| 2026-08-28 | A race is recorded as id, file string and the recording client's word, and named by the client at display | `raceFile` was being shown raw, so a French client read "NightElf"; by id it is right whoever recorded the member | `Scanners/Identity.lua`, `UI:RaceName` |
| 2026-08-28 | The summary's two profession columns are headed once, and not "Primary" | The secondaries are on the line below, so a column headed Primary was telling the truth about half of what sat under it | `Summary.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | A caption is given a right edge, or a width, or it is not allowed to hold anything this repository cannot read | A font string with a left anchor and no right one does not wrap - it grows to whatever its sentence is and keeps going past the border | `Options.lua`, `About.lua`, `Contents.lua`, `Guild.lua`, `Wide.lua`, harness *every caption long enough to be a sentence* |
| 2026-08-28 | The guild panel's two buttons are anchored to each other rather than to fixed offsets from the right | `-6` and `-122` encodes "a button that is 110 wide", which was true of the English labels and of nothing else | `Guild.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | Profession records carry the locale they were written in, and a profession whose recipes are under another language's name is reported as that rather than as never opened | A profession has no id on Era so it is keyed by its name, and a name is one language; the panel was calling a profession unopened while holding its recipes | `Scanners/Professions.lua`, `Family_UI/Professions.lua`, `L-015` |
| 2026-08-28 | Professions are keyed by skill line id everywhere - meta, payload, the recipe search and the panels - and shown in the language of whoever is reading | A name is one language and a member is only re-read on login, so the same profession under two words was two professions | `SkillLines.lua`, `Scanners/Professions.lua`, `Recipes.lua`, `Summary.lua`, `Family_UI/Professions.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | Where one recipe is held under both an identified profession and an unidentified one, the identified one is the label | "Copper Chain Belt, Runeforging" is wrong for a player, not only for a harness | `Recipes.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | Races are named from a generated `ChrRaces` table by file string, and the recording client's own word is preferred where it is in the reader's language | The file string was being shown raw and the recorded word was one language; "Scourge" is not a word the game shows anybody, and no table can gender a name the way the client already had | `addons/Family/Races.lua`, `tools/races.py`, `Family:RaceName`, `UI:RaceName` |
| 2026-08-28 | Which races are playable is read from `PlayableRaceBit`, not decided | `ClientFileString` is not unique - race 23 is `Human` too, the Gilnean one - and a list somebody remembered is the kind of thing that is wrong for one client only | `tools/races.py`, harness *only the races a player can be are in the table* |
| 2026-08-28 | Both forms of a race name are shipped, to recognise a recorded word rather than to choose between them | Era genders race names in Russian and not in German, French or Spanish, so choosing would mean showing a word some client does not use; recognising costs nothing and carries every record written before the language was | `addons/Family/Races.lua`, harness *one written before the language was recorded with it* |
| 2026-08-28 | A generated file's accessors are emitted by the generator that writes it | `Family:ProfessionName` and `Family:SkillLineFor` had been added to `SkillLines.lua` by hand, so the next `--fetch` would have deleted them and left an addon that loads and then fails at the first profession | `tools/skill-lines.py`, `tools/races.py`, harness *still carries the functions that read it* |
| 2026-08-28 | The broker bar is brought up to date whenever the database changes, without the panels' deferral | It is the one figure on screen that nobody asked to see and it was written once at login, so it disagreed with the tooltip above it and the summary behind it | `Broker.lua`, harness *says the same total the tooltip under it says* |
| 2026-08-28 | Mail in the mailbox and mail in the post are two columns of one number, not one column holding a phrase | "1 (1 in post)" is fourteen characters of a sixty-five pixel column in English and "1 (1 en el correo)" in Spanish; two numbers fit in any language because neither holds a word | `Summary.lua` `CELL.mail`, `CELL.inpost` |
| 2026-08-28 | Elapsed time is one letter of unit and matches the units the other direction uses | "19 days ago" was still wide enough to be cut to "19 days a..." in the activity row, in the language every width was measured in | `UI:Ago`, `UI:In`, `Locales/` |
| 2026-08-28 | Every column set is drawn by the harness and every cell measured against the column it was put in | Only the overview set had ever been drawn, so six of the seven were checked by looking at them in the game - which is how a truncated cell was reported from a screenshot | `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | An announcement whose sender we already hold recent data from is answered with a whispered hello, marked as a reply so nothing answers it back | The traffic-control skip was the only thing that ever went to that person, and what we hold from them says nothing about what they hold from us | `Guild.lua` `Guild:SayHelloBack`, `onHello`, spec §7 |
| 2026-08-28 | `Family:Fit` and its `PX_PER_CHAR` are removed rather than repaired: text is never cut to fit a box | It was the rejected approach sitting in the tree ready to be used, it estimated where everything beside it measures, and it was wrong - `Fit("Leatherworking", 65)` returned twelve characters of a ten-character budget, uncaught because nothing called it | `addons/Family/Locale.lua`, `UI:FitColumns` and `UI:LayOutRow` are where this is done now |
| 2026-08-28 | Every column set is drawn and measured in all five languages, not only the one the widths were chosen in | The cell-fit check ran in English, which is the shortest language and the one no translated string can fail in; five minutes of it found six overflows nobody had seen | `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | The summary's totals label sits beside the first number it labels unless it does not fit there, and then at the far left | "Grand totals" fits the column left of the money in English with six pixels spare and "Totales generales" needs twenty-nine more than exist; no translation rescues a fixed cell | `Summary.lua` `totalsRow` |
| 2026-08-28 | A recipe is named from its spell id at display, by `Family.Names:Recipe`, and the recorded word is only the fallback | The id has been recorded since recipes were, and the word was shown anyway - a list read on an English client stayed English under a profession that had just learnt to say Secourisme | `Names.lua`, `Family_UI/Professions.lua`, `Recipes.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | The same name is used for the click that selects a row, for the sort, for the search and for the crafters block - not only for what is drawn | The open trade skill window answers in the client's language, so a row carrying the recorded word searched an English list for a French string and found nothing; the crafters block compared an item name from this client against a recipe name from whoever scanned it | `Family_UI/Professions.lua`, `Recipes.lua` `Crafters` |
| 2026-08-28 | Recipe search matches the displayed name and the recorded one | A family holds lists read on other people's clients, and somebody typing their own language should not find fewer of them than somebody typing the language a list happened to be written in | `Recipes.lua`, `Family_UI/Professions.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | A recipe falls back to the name of the item it makes before it falls back to the recorded word | A live Era client gave 150 leatherworking recipes an item id each and not one spell id, so the item is the only id there is to name most recipes by | `Names.lua` `Names:Recipe` |
| 2026-08-28 | A recipe link's id is read from whichever kind of link the client returns, not from `enchant:` alone | Which kind a client hands back is not something this repository can settle, and assuming one kind cost every trade skill recipe its identity | `Scanners/Professions.lua` `idFromLink` |
| 2026-08-28 | `/family recipes` reports what each stored recipe has to be named by, and what an open window actually returns | Whether an id is in somebody's saved data is not knowable from here, and it is the difference between a display fault and a scan that has to be run again | `Family_UI/Slash.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | The item a recipe makes is the identity Era actually supplies; the spell id is preferred only where a client gives one | Measured, not assumed: `GetTradeSkillRecipeLink` returns nil on 1.15.9 and 229 recipes across three professions had an item id each and no spell id at all | `Names.lua`, `DATASOURCES.md` §2 |
| 2026-08-28 | Both recipe ids are read from both links, on both the TradeSkill and the Craft frame | Era answers `GetCraftItemLink` with an enchant link and `GetCraftRecipeLink` with nothing, so reading each call for the id it is named after left 101 enchanting recipes with no identity | `Scanners/Professions.lua`, `DATASOURCES.md` §2 |
| 2026-08-28 | `Family:ProfessionName` resolves a recorded word to its skill line before falling back to printing it | A member is only re-keyed when somebody logs in on them, so a family of 27 halfway through read half in French and half in English on one screen - a migration showing through the panel | `tools/skill-lines.py`, `SkillLines.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | A recipe named by its item asks the client to load that item and redraws when it answers | The client answers about an item only once it has loaded it, so a list of 150 came back part-answered - half a first aid list in French and half in English on one screen | `Names:Recipe`, `Family_UI/Professions.lua`, the same arrangement `Contents.lua` has |
| 2026-08-28 | Where a record was written in the reader's own language, its own words win over the item's name | The game drew that row: smelting says *Smelt Copper* and makes a Copper Bar, so naming it after its product is wrong for the one reader who could already read it | `Names:Recipe`, the same rule `Family:RaceName` follows |
| 2026-08-28 | The "recorded in another language" case is gone: records filed under a word are resolved to their skill line instead | Professions are keyed by identity now, and the flag was a property of the whole member - so a member with fishing was told fishing had been recorded in another language because something else had | `Family_UI/Professions.lua`, locale files |
| 2026-08-28 | The professions note is given a width and the height it measures, not one fixed line | It names professions and says why each is missing; unwrapped, the half that fell off the right edge was the why | `Family_UI/Professions.lua`, harness records `SetHeight` so it can be checked |
| 2026-08-28 | The recipe list's heading names its profession, like every other place that shows one | It printed the skill line the profession is keyed by, so a live client read "129 241/300" where the profession's name belongs | `Family_UI/Professions.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | A recipe waiting for a window to open is marked on its row, not only announced in a sentence | The message tells you to move the mouse to a button several inches away, and the highlight it had was the one that follows the mouse | `UI:WaitingFor`, `Family_UI/Professions.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | The harness records `SetShown` on regions as well as frames | A texture shown or hidden by a condition was invisible to every check, which is how a mark that was never drawn passed | `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | A bank bag with no name of its own is numbered as the player numbers them, not by its container id | The bank's own window is container -1 and its bags start at five, so the first bank bag was called the fifth | `Family_UI/Contents.lua` `containerName` |
| 2026-08-28 | Every sentence the slash commands print is translated in all four languages, and the harness refuses one that is not | A missing translation degrading to English is right for a label and wrong for a sentence: the values inside one are translated whatever the sentence is, so a French player was sent "seen il y a 19j, 2 container(s), meta says jamais" | `Locales/`, harness *translates every sentence the slash commands print* |
| 2026-08-28 | `MANUAL.md` stays English; the manual inside the addon is the translated one | Six thousand words in four more languages would have to track behaviour with nothing able to check that they still do, and the reader who never leaves the game already has the About tab in their language | `docs/MANUAL.md`, `About.lua`, `RELEASING.md` |
| 2026-08-28 | The manual is read against the changelog's Unreleased section at every release, as a step in `RELEASING.md` and a reminder from `release.sh` | A document cannot be checked for describing last month's behaviour, so the only guard available is a step taken while the notes are still in front of you | `docs/RELEASING.md`, `tools/release.sh` |
| 2026-08-28 | Nothing a user reads carries how Family is built: no mechanism, no internal names, no reasoning about how a fault arose, no lessons | A player knows nothing about the inside of this and should not have to; the commit, `DECISIONS.md` and `LESSONS.md` exist so that none of it needs to be in front of them | `CHANGELOG.md` header, `docs/MANUAL.md` |
| 2026-08-28 | A talent is named from the spell id its position maps to, generated into `TalentSpells.lua`; the recorded word is the fallback | The client answers about any spell for any class, which is the way round the one that would not describe another class's talents - so no names are shipped and every language the game has is covered | `tools/talents.py`, `addons/Family/TalentSpells.lua`, `Family_UI/Talents.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | The talent table is keyed by expansion rather than merged, and Mists has a table of its own shape | Era and Burning Crusade hold different talents at 32 of the 419 positions they share; Mists is a different system entirely, with an id per talent and no position to key on | `tools/talents.py`, harness *a position the two builds disagree about* |
| 2026-08-28 | Talent tree names are shipped from `TalentTab.Name_lang`, the way profession and race names are | They have no spell to be named from, and the game's own table is where the other shipped names already come from - hesitating over it was inconsistent with `SkillLines.lua` and `Races.lua`, which exist for exactly this case | `tools/talents.py`, `Family:TalentTreeName` |
| 2026-08-28 | Both sharing panels are always in the strip, both features ship off, and both switches live in Options and nowhere else | Wide Family's tab appeared only once its feature was on, so the only way to learn it existed was to read a manual. A visibility preference was tried first and taken out again: two concepts where there is one, and a panel is not the thing being switched | `Family_UI/Wide.lua`, `Family_UI/Guild.lua`, `Options.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | A panel whose feature is off explains itself and points at Options, rather than carrying a switch of its own | One place to look for a switch beats one per feature scattered across the panels they govern; and with the panels always registered, neither switch needs a reload any more | `Family_UI/Wide.lua`, `Family_UI/Guild.lua`, `Slash.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | Guild share ships off, like Wide Family | Not consent - the §7 argument stands - but a first release should not begin talking to a guild on somebody's behalf before they have asked, whatever it is saying | `Guild.lua`, specification §7 |
| 2026-08-28 | `store()` writes no default for guild share; `Enabled()` is the only answer | A value written at creation is that answer made permanent by whichever version happened to create the file | `Guild.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | A bank container's free slots are derived from what was read, not asked for | Classic Era computes the bank window's free count from 28 slots where the bank has 24, so it is out by four whatever is in it - and every slot is read anyway, so the count needs no asking and cannot then contradict the contents | `Scanners/Bank.lua`, `L-018` |
| 2026-08-28 | A bank scan records nothing unless a bank window is open, and only `BANKFRAME_OPENED` says one is | Away from a bank the client still describes the bank container, so a scan there writes a well-formed record of an empty bank over a real one - and a flag set by three events and cleared by one stays set after a missed close | `Scanners/Bank.lua`, `L-019` |
| 2026-08-28 | Entering the world clears the bank-open flag | Logging in, zoning and teleporting cannot happen with a bank window open, and a flag with one way out is a flag that stays set | `Scanners/Bank.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | Closing the bank no longer scans | The last look happened as the client was taking the bank down, which is when it answers with a container and nothing in it; every change while the window is open already arrives as a bag update | `Scanners/Bank.lua` |
| 2026-08-28 | The bank's own window is drawn with `Minimap\TRACKING\Banker`, the game's own symbol for a bank | It had no icon of its own and fell through to the backpack button, so a vault was drawn as a bag on the one panel where what a container is carries meaning; confirmed rendering on all three clients before it was written | `Family_UI/Contents.lua`, `tools/FamilyIconSheet` |
| 2026-08-28 | Wide Family reaches any realm - recorded as a measurement, not an expectation | The 1.0.0 pass linked two families on unrelated realms and they exchanged, closing specification §11.1, the last open question; it had been asked precisely because it could not be assumed | `Project high level specs.md` §11.1, `MANUAL.md` §11, `SMOKE.md` |
| 2026-08-28 | A panel's off-note is given a width and measured, and what sits under it is anchored below what it measured | The note is a sentence: one line in English, two in French, and a fixed drop drew the two through each other - the same fault the professions note had, in a panel written after it | `Family_UI/Wide.lua`, `Family_UI/Guild.lua`, harness *the box under it starts below the whole of it* |
| 2026-08-28 | A sharing panel greys its own controls while its feature is off | Neither button can do anything - no roster to filter, nothing to ask a guild nobody is speaking to - and a live-looking button that answers nothing is worse than one that says it is not available | `Family_UI/Guild.lua`, `Family_UI/Wide.lua` |
| 2026-08-29 | Guild crafters: a profession is shared per member and per profession, one tick, and that tick carries its rank, its recipe list and its cooldowns together | The unit somebody reasons about is "my Leatherworking on that character"; splitting rank from recipes doubles the grid and asks a question almost nobody has an opinion about | planned: spec §7, `Guild.lua`, `Family_UI/Guild.lua` |

| 2026-08-29 | A shared cooldown crosses as seconds remaining and is stored as a moment on arrival | Two clients need not agree on an epoch, and a duration is right whoever reads it; storing it as a moment is still the rule, because a countdown written down yesterday is wrong today | planned: `Guild.lua`, `Scanners/Professions.lua`, `Cooldowns.lua` |

| 2026-08-29 | `Guild:Forget` gains a caller - leaving a guild drops what was held for it | It has existed unused since guild share was written, so today the data survives leaving and comes back stale on a rejoin; the panel only stopped showing it because it looks up by the current guild | planned: `Guild.lua` |

Introduced by the change one commit earlier and reported from play within the hour. Of 147 makers exactly three are crafted by a profession: the Salt Shaker, the SnowMaster 9000 and a Heavy Leather Ball. Marked rather than dropped, because the table's other use is *who can make me one of these*, and there the Chronoboon belongs | `tools/made-by-item.py`, `Family/MadeByItem.lua`, `Family/Scanners/Bags.lua` |
| 2026-09-01 | An item cooldown reaches the Crafting panel only where a profession **makes the maker** and something **other than the maker's own recipe uses what it makes** | Three rejections from play in one evening, each defeating the rule before it. Then a SnowMaster 9000 passed *its product is a profession reagent* - because the engineering recipe that builds one takes Snowballs, and a SnowMaster makes Snowballs, so the product is a reagent of the very recipe that makes the thing producing it. Excluding that loop leaves exactly one item on Classic Era: the Salt Shaker, whose Refined Deeprock Salt goes into Cured Rugged Hide. On the later builds it leaves the Mote and Eternal conversions and nothing else | `tools/made-by-item.py`, `Family/MadeByItem.lua`, `Family/Cooldowns.lua` `IsCraftingItem`, `Family/Scanners/Bags.lua` |
| 2026-09-01 | The rule is applied where a cooldown is **shown** as well as where it is recorded | `cooldownItems` is learned and never pruned, so an item that qualified under an older rule stays on disk - which is how a Super Snapper FX survived a fix that only changed what gets written. Filtering at the point of display makes a stale record harmless without a migration | `Family/Cooldowns.lua` `Crafting`, `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-09-01 | The harness's item-cooldown fixtures use the **real Salt Shaker id**, not a stand-in | An invented id cannot pass a rule that asks the client's own tables what a thing makes and who uses it, so every check in that block would have been exercising a member with no cooldowns at all while appearing to pass. | 2026-09-01 | Whether an item's cooldown is a **crafting** cooldown is answered **per expansion**, from `Family.CraftingItems`, and the maker's wait must clear the same six hours the bag scanner uses | The union was carrying a property onto builds that do not have it: a Mote of Fire has a third of a second on Mists and the *no cooldown* sentinel on Burning Crusade, and Mote-to-Primal was drawn on the Crafting panel on both. Reported from play. The floor is the bag scanner's own, which already refuses anything under six hours, so the two halves of the same judgement now agree. What is left is the salt shaker on Classic Era and Burning Crusade, and nothing at all on Mists - where its cooldown was removed. The per-entry marker is gone: one source of truth, read through `Family.Capabilities.expansion` as `RecipeCooldowns` is | `tools/made-by-item.py`, `Family/MadeByItem.lua`, `Family/Cooldowns.lua` `IsCraftingItem`, `Family/Scanners/Bags.lua` |
| 2026-09-01 | Who already knows a recipe is matched on **the spell the item teaches**, from a generated `Family/RecipeTeaches.lua`, with the name test kept as the fallback | The name test cannot work for enchanting: the trade skill window abbreviates, so `Ench. de bottes (Agilite superieure)` is not a suffix of `Formule : Enchantement de bottes (Agilite superieure)` and an enchanter who knew the recipe was offered it as one to learn. Reported from play. Both sides carried ids the whole time - the recipe list records the taught spell, and `ItemEffect` plus `SpellEffect` say which spell an item teaches. Per expansion, because item 23133 teaches different spells on Burning Crusade and Mists | `tools/recipe-teaches.py`, `Family/RecipeTeaches.lua`, `Family/Recipes.lua` `TaughtBy`, `Crafters`, `KnowersOf`, `Family.toc`, `.gitignore` |
| 2026-09-01 | A recipe item teaches in **two shapes** and the generator accepts both | Era's items teach through effect 36 with a trigger; Burning Crusade and Mists carry two rows, a generic spell 483 with no trigger and the craft spell itself. Reading only the first gave 1008 items on Era and **none at all** on the other two builds - a generator answering nothing for two thirds of the clients, which is the quiet failure this project keeps refusing. The refusal that caught the third build's disagreement is what turned the union into a per-expansion table | `tools/recipe-teaches.py`, `docs/DATASOURCES.md` |
| 2026-09-01 | The crafters-block fixture uses the **client's real ids**: item 2881 is *Plans: Runed Copper Breastplate*, teaching spell 2667 and making item 2864 | It claimed 2881 was *Plans: Copper Chain Belt*, its recipe was spell 2661 and its product item 2864 - three ids and a name that agree with nothing in the client. | 2026-08-31 | The same join is read to its end and used in **two lanes**: recipe item -> the spell it teaches, and recipe item -> the item that spell makes | One lane answers for enchanting and for nothing else on Classic Era, because the two windows on that client carry opposite halves - a trade skill record holds an item id and no spell, the Craft frame holds the enchant's spell and no item. Proposed from play as *collect the data and use the table left to right or right to left*, which is what it needed. `SpellEffect` effect 24 supplies the product from rows the generator already walks, so the second lane costs one column. Measured: 908 of Era's 1008 recipe items name a product, 96 of the hundred that do not are enchanting, and the four left are Tinker schematics, which create nothing. The generator refuses a spell that makes two different items rather than picking one - a wrong product is not a miss, it is another recipe's answer. The guild half of the block is asked by both ids too, where it had only ever been asked by name | `tools/recipe-teaches.py`, `Family/RecipeTeaches.lua`, `Family/Recipes.lua` `Makes`, `Crafters`, `Family_UI/Tooltip.lua`, `tests/Harness.lua`, `docs/DATASOURCES.md` |
| 2026-08-31 | `.pkgmeta` externals are keyed **relative to the package directory** - `Libs/LibStub`, not `Family/Libs/LibStub` - and the harness now checks that against both `.toc` files | Written with the package name in front, the packager put all three libraries at `Family/Family/Libs`, one level below where the `.toc` looks, so every release from v1.0.0 to v1.4.0 shipped with Wide Family and Guild share unable to send anything. Reported from play by somebody installing from CurseForge on another account and another computer, which is the only configuration that could show it: `FetchLibs.sh` puts the libraries where the `.toc` expects them, so this machine has never had the layout players get. Confirmed by listing the published zips - every one of the five - and the correction confirmed by running the packager locally against a clone with `-d -l -z`, which fetches and moves without tagging or uploading. L-038 | `.pkgmeta`, `tests/Harness.lua`, `docs/LESSONS.md` |
| 2026-09-02 | A linked family is whispered on **any character they have told us about**, not only on the ones we have heard from | A family is a person and any one of their characters answers for all of them, which is the whole design of Wide Family - but the address list was `link.characters`, which is filled by `noteHeard` and therefore holds only characters that have already sent something here. A character created after the link arrives as a *member*: visible on the panel, tickable as a sibling, readable in the summary, and never whispered. So an update said *none of their 1 character is online* while the player sat on one of the other five. Reported from play. Members are keyed "Name-Realm", which is the form a whisper wants, and they rank below the heard-from because being heard from is evidence of having been online while being shared is only evidence of existing. The same list now answers "is this refusal about this link" - matched against the heard-from alone, a refusal naming a member belonged to no link, so nothing moved on and nothing was said | `Family/Wide.lua` `candidates`, `reachableName`, `characterCount`, the absent handler, `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-09-03 | The item lane of `RecipeCooldowns` is keyed by **skill line as well as by item**, and a match must agree about both | What a recipe makes does not say which profession made it: a Gold Bar is smelted by a miner for nothing and transmuted by an alchemist on a day's wait, and a Truesilver Bar is the same pair. Reported from play. A spell id is unambiguous and its lane is unchanged. | 2026-09-03 | A cooldown mark is **carried forward only where Family watched it run**; one derived from a table is re-derived at every scan | Both were one flag, and carrying it forward by name made every answer permanent - including a wrong one. | 2026-09-03 | An item cooldown whose remaining time exceeds its own duration is **refused**, not recorded and not clamped | `start` is a moment that has passed, so `start + duration - now` can never exceed `duration`; more than that means the client answered with a start in the future, which it does at login before its cooldown clock has settled. Reported from play, and the rescan was the clue: the arithmetic was right and one of its inputs was not. Refused rather than clamped because a clamp invents a moment out of a number already known to be wrong, and the list is rewritten whole at every scan - so a refusal costs one scan and clears the bad record. | 2026-09-03 | CurseForge is shown `RELEASE-NOTES.md`, written by `release.sh` with this version's section alone, rather than the whole `CHANGELOG.md` | `manual-changelog` names a file and the packager sends it whole, so every release since 1.0.0 published 58 kB of history under an empty Unreleased heading. Found by opening the published GitHub release while answering a question about how notes get there - the same way the missing libraries were found, and the second time this month that a claim about a published artifact was wrong because nothing had opened one. | 2026-09-04 | Published pages (Wowhead, the wikis, patch notes) are read for **rules and shapes** and never harvested for data; `wago.tools` is the only sanctioned machine-readable source | The practice was already there and the reason was not written down anywhere - `DATASOURCES` records that the Chronoboon names were used *only as search terms* and every id came from the client, but nothing said why. Their terms are about method rather than volume: a fact is free to restate with the source beside it, a table is the product of somebody's work, and a scraper pointed at those sites is the thing the terms name. Recorded after Alberto restated it from memory and it turned out to exist nowhere. Practical note discovered in the same turn: Wowhead does not render for an automated fetcher at all, so reading it means a person pasting what matters | `docs/DATASOURCES.md` |
| 2026-09-04 | The honor rules section carries an explicit **re-read at a new build** instruction, because nothing can re-run it | The rules of the game are a per-build fact exactly as `SpellCooldowns` is - 1.14.4 replaced the ranking system every guide still describes, inside one expansion - but a page is not a generator: it cannot refuse, print a count, or notice that a number moved. Where a source cannot shout at a new build, the document says so instead of trusting that somebody will remember | `docs/DATASOURCES.md` |
| 2026-09-04 | Honor is **deferred to a build of its own**, after the other seven backlog entries have shipped and been released | It is the only entry that needs research, then a model, then code: the research found three systems sharing a word - fourteen ranks and a 500,000 weekly cap on Era, a currency with no ranks and arena points on Burning Crusade, two currencies and a per-character conquest cap on Mists - so *how much is missing for the weekly cap* means something different on each build and one column would be wrong on two of three. Held last so that it does not hold the rest behind it. Alberto's call, and the reason it is a good one is that everything else is either a panel over data already stored or a small self-contained addition | `docs/BACKLOG.md` |
| 2026-09-04 | LibDBIcon is **used where the player already has it and never shipped**; the hand-written minimap button stays as the fallback | Addons that collect minimap buttons collect what LibDBIcon registered, so Family's own button sat outside the bag while two other addons on the same minimap were gathered - reported from play, with Family named in somebody else's complaint. Bundling it was the obvious answer and the licences refuse it: LibDBIcon's terms forbid redistributing a stand-alone version without written permission from the Ace3 lead, and the LibDataBroker it hard-requires states no licence in its source, its README or its project page, where the field reads All Rights Reserved. Neither can travel inside a zip that promises GPL-3.0-or-later to whoever receives it, whatever the rest of the ecosystem does. Reading a library another addon has already loaded is not redistributing it, so `.pkgmeta` fetches nothing new and neither `.toc` loads anything new - and the harness checks both, because the cheap way to lose this property is to add the external later without remembering why it was absent. LibDBIcon's saved position is an angle in degrees, which is the unit the hand-written button had been storing, so it is seeded rather than defaulted and nobody's button moves. Alberto's call, taken as the thing to try first: if a player who has no collector still wants Family in one, the two rejected options - shipping the libraries anyway, or asking their authors for permission in writing - are still there | `Family_UI/Broker.lua` `GiveButtonToCollector`, `BuildMinimapButton`, `SetMinimapShown`, `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-09-04 | Both `.toc` files carry `## IconTexture`, pointing at the **one** icon `Family_UI/Textures/Family.tga` rather than a copy each | The addon list drew a red question mark against Family and against Family (interface), beside addons that had a picture - reported from play with the panel open. The mark already exists and is already loaded by the minimap button and the broker bars, so this is one line per file and no new art. The data layer names Family_UI's path deliberately: two addons that always ship together are one thing to the player, and a second copy of a 64x64 file is a second thing to forget when the first is redrawn. Confirmed in play the same day: both rows draw the mark. `IconTexture` was added to the addon list in 10.1.0 and all three pinned clients are built later than that, which was the reason to expect it - but a texture path is the thing the client accepts without ever saying whether it found anything (HANDOFF §2), so the list itself was the only place the answer could come from, and it was opened before this was written down as working. Two things about a texture are checkable without the client and both are now checked, because a screenshot taken once does not catch a regression six months later: that the `.toc` path and the `ICON` constant still name the same file, and that the file is a TGA this client can decode - uncompressed, 32-bit, power-of-two sides, none of which an image editor mentions when it silently gives you something else | `Family/Family.toc`, `Family_UI/Family_UI.toc`, `tools/FamilyIconSheet/FamilyIconSheet.toc`, `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-09-04 | The minimap tick box toggles **the frame LibDBIcon hands back**, not `lib:Show` | `lib:Show` is not only a show: it calls the library's own `updatePosition`, which does `ClearAllPoints` and re-anchors the button to the minimap's centre. A collector that had taken the button into a bar of its own would have seen it torn back onto the circle by a tick box that only ever meant *draw it* - so re-ticking the option would have undone the feature the option belongs to. Found by reading the library's source while chasing a different report, not from play, which is the argument for reading a library before calling it rather than after. `lib:Hide` has no such tail and would have been safe on its own, but the pair is written one way so that the next reader does not have to work out which half was deliberate. `GetMinimapButton` is checked for before it is called, because it is one more thing assumed about something another addon loaded (§2.3), and the library's own calls stay as the fallback | `Family_UI/Broker.lua` `SetMinimapShown`, `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-09-04 | The button is **registered with a collector only while the option is on**, and a toggle that does not take is read back and said aloud | Registering is one-way: LibDBIcon registers and never unregisters. HidingBar - the collector in the report - takes buttons parented to the minimap *by name* rather than through the broker object, its own settings show `Grab addon buttons on minimap` on and `Add buttons from DataBroker` off, and once it has one it keeps it: `button:Hide()` left `IsShown()` **true on the same frame**, measured in play. So nothing Family calls can take that button off the screen, and the fix is not to call harder. While the option is off nothing is handed over, so there is nothing on any bar to argue with; the setting is then honoured completely from the next login. For the flip that happens while a bar is already holding it, the result is read back rather than assumed - §2.3 pointed at another addon - and the player is told once, because a tick box that silently does nothing is worse than a tick box that explains itself. Known asymmetry, stated rather than fixed: the hand-written button on a client with no LibDBIcon is still created and hidden, and a collector that grabs hidden minimap children could hold that one too. It is the behaviour every release since 1.0.0 has had, no report names it, and changing it on a guess would be a second untested path | `Family_UI/Broker.lua` `GiveButtonToCollector`, `registerWithCollector`, `SetMinimapShown`, `Family/Locales/*.lua`, `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-09-04 | The summary's filters **compose with the column set's own narrowing**, are not remembered between sessions, and never hide a member over something Family was not told | `gather` already took a predicate and the set already owned it, so the choice was replace or compose - and replacing would have made a name typed on Crafting list the whole family, which is the opposite of narrowing. Not remembered because the two are different kinds of switch: the side buttons beside them are a preference and are kept, while a name in a box is a question asked once, and a panel that opens tomorrow showing four of forty because of a word nobody remembers typing is a panel that looks broken. A record with no level or class in it passes both filters - **a guard on an invariant, not a case**, and the row said the opposite until Alberto asked when it ever happens. Measured: `Identity.lua` and `Bags.lua` are the only writers that create a member and both write level and class together, every other scanner merges onto a record that exists, `addLetter` refuses a key it has no meta for, `Wide.lua` has sent both in IDENTITY since the first commit, and nothing clears either - so it never happens. Kept anyway because it costs one `and` and the failure it prevents is a member silently vanishing from a panel; not kept as evidence of anything. Worth noting for whoever reads `factionShown` beside it: that guard carries the same justification and nobody has measured it either. Two numbers rather than a bracket list, because brackets would have to be right on three clients whose ceilings are 60, 70 and 90. The count of what is hidden is shown only while a filter is on, and counted against what the set would show rather than against the family, or Crafting would report a filter nobody set | `Family_UI/Summary.lua`, `Family/Locales/*.lua`, `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-09-04 | Every field name in Wide Family's `CATEGORIES` and `IDENTITY` must be **a name something else in the addon writes**, checked against the sources the `.toc` loads | The mapping is written by hand beside the scanners on purpose - §6, so that adding a scanner cannot quietly widen what a link already agreed to - and the price of that is a list of bare strings with nothing joining it to reality. Two of them were wrong from the first commit: `mailExpires` where the scanner writes `mailExpiresBy`, and `auctionSeen` where it writes `auctionsSeen`. Every sibling ever shared arrived without a mail expiry and without an auction age, and nothing looked broken at either end - the grant was there, the letters came through, and only the figure saying *when you lose them* was missing. Found while measuring for the login notice that would have been built on top of it. The check matches on word boundaries rather than substrings, because `mailExpires` is a prefix of `mailExpiresBy` and a plain find would have called the typo written. Raised as a reserved question in the same breath and withdrawn, because the question was wrong: `auctionsSelling` and `auctionsBidding` are named in no category, but sharing them would widen nothing - the `auctions` payload already carries both lists in full, and a count of a list somebody already has is not a fact about them they did not have. Measured after asking rather than before, which is the mistake worth remembering here. They are also read by nothing at all: two writes in `Scanners/Auctions.lua` and no reader anywhere, because the panel counts live auctions out of the payload instead. So they are not shared, and what they actually are is two dead fields written at every auction house scan | `Family/Wide.lua` `CATEGORIES`, `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-09-04 | The mail notice **names the character being played**, unlike the cooldown notice beside it, and treats *already gone* as a different fact from *expiring now* | The cooldown notice leaves the current character out and says why: a transmute you can cast is on your own action bar and being told about it is what gets an addon switched off. Mail is not that - the game gives the player an envelope on the minimap and never says when its contents go, so the character standing there is exactly as badly informed as the other thirty-nine. `UI:In` returns *now* for a moment that has passed, which for mail would send somebody running to a mailbox that is already empty, so `Mail:Expiring` returns an `expired` flag and the notice says *already gone*. **Siblings too, named by their family** - built the other way first and reversed the same day. The reasoning for leaving them out was that a mailbox in somebody else's family is a problem the reader cannot go and fix, which turned out to be an assumption about how Wide Family is used rather than a fact about it: Alberto's answer was that linked families coordinate. Whose a character is is never merged away (§6), so the family name travels with the line. A shared character whose link last exchanged before `mailExpiresBy` was sent under the right name carries no expiry and is simply absent until they share again, which is §2.2 and not something to paper over. Fired a second after the cooldown line, because two sentences in the same instant read as one wall and neither gets looked at | `Family/Scanners/Mail.lua` `Expiring`, `Family_UI/Slash.lua`, `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-09-04 | The warning period is **a number the player types, bounded 1-30, default 3**, and the options schema grew a `number` field rather than the row growing a special case | Family's first setting that is not a yes or a no. A box rather than a slider: a slider has to be dragged to a value it never quite lands on, and the two numbers a player wants here are the one they were told and the one they already had in mind. Bounded because mail lives thirty days - a longer period names every letter in the game and nought names only what is already lost - and a refused value is put back rather than kept, because a control still showing a number that is not in force is a control lying about the setting. A stored number outside the bounds falls back to three rather than being honoured, which is what a saved variables file from another build or edited by hand will hand us | `Family_UI/Options.lua` `SWITCHES`, `Family_UI/Slash.lua` `MailNoticeDays`, `SetMailNoticeDays`, `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-09-04 | The mail notice is **one character to a line** under a heading, and every one of them is listed | Every entry carries a name, a realm and sometimes a family, and a family of forty with the warning set wide is all of that run together - a wall of text at login is a thing people switch off rather than read. Asked for from play, after the single line was seen working. It also removed a fault rather than only a nuisance: the first cut at this capped the *characters* in one line against a byte budget, and a mutation that measured the coloured text instead of the plain text - twelve bytes a reader never sees, per entry - was the one mutation in the set that no check caught. One name to a line has no such arithmetic in it. It was capped at ten with the remainder counted for half an hour, and reversed: a character left off is a character whose mail goes without being mentioned, and a number at the bottom does not prevent that. The length is the player's to control and there is a control for it - the notice switches off and the warning period is a number in the options panel - so a list somebody finds too long has a shorter one behind it that costs them nothing | `Family_UI/Slash.lua` `MailNotice`, `Family/Locales/*.lua`, `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-09-04 | ~~A name carries its realm **whenever the account has more than one realm in it**~~ — **superseded 2026-09-05**, see the row at the foot of this table | The old rule was the clash alone, and its reasoning was width: a realm on every name spends a third of a tooltip line saying something nearly always obvious. That holds exactly as long as there is one realm to be on. Reported from play by somebody whose characters are spread about - *Tossica has one* is unambiguous and still does not say which realm to log into, and a tooltip that leaves it out is a trip to the wrong auction house. So the test moved from the list to the account, which costs a walk over meta on each call - the same walk the summary does to draw itself - and no cache, because invalidating one on every scan, share and removal buys a saving nobody can measure on a hover. Changed in `NamesOf` rather than in the tooltip: the file already says a search result needs the same treatment and neither should have its own idea of it. | 2026-09-04 | A linked family can be given a **local alias**, which replaces the label everywhere and the address nowhere | A link is made with a character, so `link.name` is a character name - *Smith-PyrewoodVillage* - and it was doing two jobs: the string every panel and tooltip labels their members with, and the name a whisper is addressed to. The alias takes the first and must never touch the second, because a whisper to a name nobody has fails silently and Wide Family would simply stop updating. `Wide:Called(link)` is the one reader, and the two producers - `Wide:Siblings` and `Index:Owners` - go through it, which fixes every consumer at once; `candidates(link)` deliberately does not. Never sent: it is our name for their family, not theirs, and `offering` builds what leaves from a fixed field list this is not on. The real name is shown in grey beside the alias rather than hidden, because it is the address and an address nobody can see is one nobody can check. Both producers are checked, and the tooltip one only because a mutation putting the raw name back there went unnoticed with every other check green | `Family/Wide.lua` `Called`, `Alias`, `SetAlias`, `Family/Index.lua`, `Family_UI/Wide.lua`, `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-09-04 | Three new share categories - **character, currencies, world buffs** - and the crafting cooldowns folded into the existing **professions** | Measured rather than guessed at, and the first measurement was wrong: scanning only `Summary.lua` found twelve meta fields a shared character could never fill, and scanning every reader found twenty-three. The user-visible ones were played, rested, xpMax, guild, guildless, hearth, hearthID, currencies, currenciesSeen, boons, banked, questMax, and the crafting-cooldown triple. Three categories rather than one, for the reason §6 gives for keeping Equipment out of Possessions: somebody will say where their alts are bound and not how long they have played. The cooldowns are the exception and Alberto's call - they go into a category that already exists, so a link that granted Professions starts sending them without being asked again, on the argument that *when can this person make it* is the question Professions is granted to answer. Nothing else widens: an unticked category sends nothing. `questMax` went into Quests for the same reason and with no argument to have | `Family/Wide.lua` `CATEGORIES`, `docs/Project high level specs.md`, `tests/Harness.lua` |

The body of a binding is a string the client compiles on a key press and nothing else in the addon ever compiles it, so a typo there is a syntax error the player meets mid-fight; the harness loads it with `loadstring`, runs it, and checks the window opened and closed. The bound is `ATTACHMENTS_MAX_RECEIVE`, the client's own constant, because a number written here is right on the client it was written against and quietly short on the next. `attachmentsExpected` is kept so that the next disagreement says so rather than looking complete, and it is said in the debug log rather than guessed at or filled in. One account with alts on two realms of a group is enough - they can mail each other and behave in every in-game way as though they shared a realm, and they are two different characters - so neither Wide Family nor a second account is needed for the ambiguity, and the realm that never needs saying is the one under your feet. What changes is how much of the old thing does. | 2026-09-08 | **`/family widetime`**, a diagnostic that times a Wide Family exchange on the client that is complaining about it | A French user with two accounts and eighty characters between them reports the game pausing at every login while Wide Family is on. The shape of the answer is known from the code - an exchange builds every granted member's offering, which decodes that member's record, then folds the built table byte by byte to see whether it changed - and measured off-client here at about 350 ms for forty modest members, which scales with both the count and the size. What none of that gives is **their** number, and the snippet sent by hand printed nothing at all, which is what a silent error looks like with `scriptErrors` off. So the measurement goes in the addon where it can be run by a player: three timings per link, building and fingerprinting - what a login pays - beside marking, which is the same question answered from the stored string without decoding anybody, and is what the fix would spend instead. Nothing is sent, and the checks count the calls rather than reading the printed line, because the line names all three whatever it did | `addons/Family_UI/Slash.lua`, four locales, `tests/Harness.lua`, `docs/MANUAL.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` |\n| 2026-09-08 | A Wide Family exchange decides **what is worth sending before it builds anything**, from the record as it sits on disk rather than from the built offering | Reported from play as the game pausing at every login on two accounts with eighty characters between them, and measured on Alberto's own client with `/family widetime`: fifteen members, **213-301 ms** of fingerprinting against **12 ms** of marking - about eighteen to one - with building reading nought because the records were already decoded. The fingerprint is paid at every exchange and there is one at every login, on both sides, so forty members is most of a second and eighty is more. The mark is now the payload's own mark (`Database:PayloadMark`, which the login walk already used), the meta fields that go on the wire, and the granted category ids - all had without decoding. A held-back member is never built at all, so the decode goes with it. Nothing about the wire changes: the same members are offered and the same ones held. Three things still mean *send* - a payload that is not a string, a member with no `seen` (whose offering carries `time()` and so differed from itself every time already), and anything that answers nothing. `PayloadMark` gained a mark for **a record with no payload at all**, which is a stable fact and used to answer the same nil as *cannot be worked out*. One-off: the stored marks change recipe, so the first exchange after the update resends everybody once and then settles - bandwidth, throttled, not a freeze | `addons/Family/Wide.lua`, `addons/Family/Database.lua`, `tests/Harness.lua`, `CHANGELOG.md` |\n| 2026-09-08 | `/family widetime` also reports **what deciding costs today**, and how many members come back as nothing to do | The first client to run it either side of the fix read 301 ms before and 206 ms after, and was right to: the diagnostic builds an offering and folds it on purpose, because that is what an exchange used to spend and seeing the number is what made the case - but the exchange no longer goes that way, so the report described a road nothing drives down any more and looked untouched by the very change it argued for. `Wide:MarkCost` is the marking pass on its own, building nothing and sending nothing, and its line is what falls. The check reads the two counts out of the printed line rather than the word *unchanged*, because the first version passed with the count replaced by nought | `addons/Family/Wide.lua`, `addons/Family_UI/Slash.lua`, four locales, `tests/Harness.lua`, `docs/MANUAL.md` |\n| 2026-09-08 | An exchange asks **whether there is anybody to send to before it builds anything** | Read out of a live log: *0 of 15 unchanged*, followed by *none of Serena's 7 characters are online*. Both are correct and they explain each other - marks are remembered only once the client has taken the message, by design, so an exchange that never left leaves nothing to compare against next time, and every login rebuilds everything for a whisper with nowhere to go. That is the ordinary case for somebody who plays two accounts one at a time. `send` already asked the question and answered it with the same sentence; it asked after the offering was assembled, which is the expensive half. The sentence moved into a helper so the two callers cannot word it differently. Nothing about the answer changes | `addons/Family/Wide.lua`, `tests/Harness.lua` |\n| 2026-09-08 | `/family widetime` says **what an exchange costs now first**, and prints the old cost underneath as a comparison | The output listed building and fingerprinting first because that is the order they were written in, and both people who read it - including the addon's author - took those numbers for the current cost and concluded the fix had done nothing. They are the old road, walked on purpose so there is something to compare against. A diagnostic that has to be explained in a chat message is not one, so the line now reads *Now: marking 12 ms, 15 of 15 unchanged* with the old figures in grey beneath it | `addons/Family_UI/Slash.lua`, four locales, `docs/MANUAL.md` |\n| 2026-09-08 | **Both Update now buttons say what is already in flight instead of queueing a second copy**, and the panels carry the count while it drains | Specification §6, lines 719-721: *the interface reports progress rather than appearing to hang*. It was written and never built, and the audit found the shape of what that costs: nothing in the Wide chain held a *already sending* flag, `link.lastAsked` is written at `Wide.lua:768` and read nowhere, and a second press rebuilt the whole offering with `full = true` and queued every piece **behind** the first copy - doubling the wait it was pressed to shorten and paying the packing cost again in one frame. Nothing was corrupted; the reply said *Sent*, which invites a third press. The two panels are not the same shape and do not get the same sentence: Wide's minutes are its own outgoing queue (`Comm:Pending`), Guild's are N guildmates' replies arriving (`Comm:Waiting`, which was defined and called by nothing at all until now). Both sentences say what the number really counts - one queue for everything, transfers from anybody - because a count that named the link would be a claim the code cannot back. **Pressable and answering rather than greyed**, which is the argument `Family/Guild.lua` already makes about an Update now that says *no need*: a greyed button reads as broken. Each panel re-arms its own repaint while something is in flight, because a draining queue writes no record and nothing else would redraw it | `addons/Family_UI/Wide.lua`, `addons/Family_UI/Guild.lua`, four locales, `tests/Harness.lua`, `docs/BACKLOG.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` |\n| 2026-09-08 | An exchange **packs a dozen members at a time at deflate level 1**, and a member's payload mark is **folded once a session** rather than once per link | Alberto asked what the worst plausible case costs, and it was measured rather than guessed: fifteen links of 210 characters, half heavy. Packing 210 in one bundle is 2.5 s on this machine and something like seven on a client - and unlike a login it lands whenever the other family comes online, in the middle of play. Eighteen batches of twelve at level 1 cost 122 ms a frame instead, about four tenths of a second there, for 43% more bytes on a transfer that takes minutes either way (6 min to 9). Nothing about the protocol changes: the far side merges `members` and forgets on `offering`, so a partial `members` was already correct on every client that exists, and every batch carries the whole offering list. A batch's marks are written as that batch is queued, so an interrupted job leaves the rest unmarked and the next exchange offers them again - losing work is safe, believing something was sent is not. The **mark cache** is the other half: with fifteen links the same member was folded fifteen times in one second, 3,150 folds of 210 records at every login, measured at 158 ms here and something like half a second to two and a half on a client. It is dropped when the record is written, which the walk's own check for a record that changed only in the middle already proves | `addons/Family/Wide.lua`, `addons/Family/Database.lua`, `addons/Family/Codec.lua`, `tests/Harness.lua`, `docs/DATASOURCES.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` |\n| 2026-09-08 | Each addon message is **cut to the room its own header leaves**, rather than to a flat 200 characters | Alberto asked whether the channel could be made faster. Half of that question is free and this is it: the game carries 255, Family's header measures 11 to 23 for every kind and every plausible id, and the piece was a flat 200 - a seventh of every message empty, on the slowest thing in the addon. Sizing each piece by the header it will carry is 14 to 15% fewer messages for the same bytes at the same rate: 11 MB goes from 96 minutes of queue to 83. `LIMIT` keeps three characters back from 255 because what a client does with a message of exactly its limit is the one number here nobody has measured, and being wrong truncates silently - it is one constant and a comment saying who may raise it. The other half of the question, sending **more messages a second**, is not taken: ten a second is what the community's throttling library settled on, this repository has no measurement of the client's real ceiling, and the way it fails is the server dropping messages or disconnecting rather than a slow panel. The signal for an adaptive rate does exist - `sendRaw` records what the client answered to every hand-off and tells a refusal from a silence - so it is backlog 40 with the probe written down rather than a guess taken now | `addons/Family/Comm.lua`, `tests/Harness.lua`, `docs/DATASOURCES.md`, `docs/BACKLOG.md`, `CHANGELOG.md` |\n| 2026-09-08 | `Comm:Send` takes an **`onSent` callback**, called once when the client has taken every piece of a message and refused none — and never for one abandoned part way | Queued is not sent. The outgoing queue is memory and Wide Family's marks are a saved variable, so a logout half way through a transfer kept the marks and lost the messages; the same hole opens without a logout every time `AbandonTo` drops a queue for somebody who has gone offline. The channel acknowledges nothing (§11.1), so this is the strongest signal there is: *the client took all of it*. Three checks and four mutations | `addons/Family/Comm.lua`, `tests/Harness.lua`, L-064 |
| 2026-09-08 | A Wide Family **mark is written from that callback**, and a batch job that is abandoned **takes back the marks it wrote** | The mark is a claim about the other side's disk; a claim made from our own queue is a claim about nothing. `Comm` can empty the queue when the client refuses a name and it cannot unwrite our marks, so `Wide:AbandonBatches` does — called from the absent handler before the next character of theirs is tried, so the exchange that follows offers the members the last one only thought it had sent | `addons/Family/Wide.lua`, `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-09-08 | **A member crosses the wire carrying its mark**, and a `want` carries `have` — the marks of every member of theirs we hold. The answer is the difference | The side that has the records is the side that can say what it holds; ours can only say what it queued. This is what makes a transfer interrupted by a logout, a disconnect or an alt-f4 *resume* rather than restart or silently lose members, and it means the marks in `link.sent` no longer have to be right — only cheap. A protocol change inside §6, put to Alberto rather than taken: two new fields, both ignored by an older Family, and a `want` with no `have` is answered in full exactly as before | `addons/Family/Wide.lua`, `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-09-08 | **`onWant` answers through the batching path and the marks**, where it used to build every granted member into one unbatched body | It ran at every exchange and there is one at every login of either side, so it undid the delta and the batching for the commonest case there is — the two savings measured the day before. It was also what had been hiding the marks-on-queue fault, by resending everything often enough to repair it | `addons/Family/Wide.lua`, `tests/Harness.lua`, L-064 |
| 2026-09-08 | On a client with **no `C_Timer`**, `drain` reschedules itself whenever anything is left rather than only when something is held back | Found by a check watching a four-piece message all the way out: the canary holds a bulk transfer after its first piece, the retry then sends what one pass allows, and `held` is nought because the pass stopped on its own count — so nothing was rescheduled and the tail of every bulk message sat in the queue for the session. It cannot happen where `C_Timer` exists, which is all three clients, which is exactly why it was never seen | `addons/Family/Comm.lua`, `tests/Harness.lua` |
| 2026-09-09 | `WIDE-TRANSFER.md` §7 **corrected the same day it was written**: realm reach is settled, and faction is the boundary nothing has measured | The first draft repeated §6's *not true of every pairing on every client*, which is older than §11.1 and vaguer than what the 1.0.0 pass established — two families on unrelated realms exchanged. Naming the wrong uncertainty is worse than naming none: it makes a settled fact look shaky and leaves the real gap, a cross-faction whisper, unwritten. Backlog 44 is the probe | `docs/WIDE-TRANSFER.md`, `docs/BACKLOG.md` |
| 2026-09-09 | Three probes written and **not run**: which ways of ending a session write the saved variables (42), whether a link can cross factions (44); and one proposal held for Alberto — a prompt `got` acknowledgement (43) | All three came out of one question about what is guaranteed. Two are measurements this repository can take and has not, and the answer to neither changes any code — 42 changes what the manual can tell a player about closing the game, 44 changes what the panel says when a link cannot work. The third is a protocol change inside §6 and therefore Alberto's, not mine | `docs/BACKLOG.md` |
| 2026-09-09 | A **login announces and pushes nothing**, and that is now checked rather than only true | Asked by Alberto as a compound case: both sides leave mid-transfer and the receiver returns first. The answer turns entirely on this - the side that comes back **last** is the side that restarts a transfer, because what restarts one is the other side hearing an announcement and beginning an exchange whose `want` pulls the remainder out. Two checks, counted by who was whispered rather than by kind so that a guild announcement in the same second is not read as this pushing data; both mutations caught. The corollary is written down beside them: with automatic exchange off on the side already there, an interrupted transfer waits for *Update now* and not for a login | `tests/Harness.lua`, `docs/WIDE-TRANSFER.md` §5 |
| 2026-09-09 | `WIDE-TRANSFER.md` gains §7, **what can jam a transfer and how to get it moving** — one genuine jam, three that read like one, and the escape hatch named | Asked directly. It is the suspected cross-faction shape, which is why backlog 44 is worth running. | 2026-09-09 | Backlog 45: ***Update now* should refuse for this link, not for the whole queue** — proposed, not built | The button correctly never greys (a greyed control reads as broken, §6). It also asks `Comm` and not `Wide`, so `Wide:Batching` is never consulted. It matters because that button is the only caller that asks for `full`, and therefore the only escape from the jam above | `docs/BACKLOG.md` |
| 2026-09-09 | **Backlog 45 built:** *Update now* refuses for its own link rather than for the whole queue, through `Comm:PendingTo` and `Wide:InFlight`, and the link's status line counts its own traffic instead of everybody's | The old guard asked `Comm:Pending()` - every link plus the guild - and then said *what you are asking for is already on its way*, which is false for a link merely waiting behind somebody else's transfer; fifteen large links make that ninety minutes of it. It matters because that button is the only caller that asks for `full` and therefore the only way out of the one jam `WIDE-TRANSFER.md` §7 names. Dropped, Growl read as *never priced*, which is a different sentence from *free* and the wrong one — and it was the ability that left a pet's points not adding up. | 2026-09-09 | **The release notes are rewritten for the person deciding whether to install Family**, and the changelog's own voice rule now says how long one may be | Alberto: the notes are unreadable, too detailed, and win no users. 2.0.0 was 107 entries over 600 lines — every change that landed, at the length it was understood in, in the order it happened. The rule that produced it was right about voice and silent about length, ordering and headings, so it has been extended: a sentence at the top saying what the release is for, headings that name the thing itself rather than *Added* and *Fixed*, biggest change first, one sentence a bullet, related changes as one entry. Those headings are also the only search surface a release has — somebody looking for an alt manager types *profession cooldown* or *bank alt*, not *Fixed*. Nothing is lost: the detail is in the commits, `DECISIONS.md` and `LESSONS.md`, which is where this file's own preamble has always said it belongs. | 2026-09-10 | **The full auction read starts with one query and a comparison, not with a walk** | Slice 3 of the price work was picked up and stopped on its first step, which is why it was taken in that order. A page walk has to vary `page`, and where `page` sits in `QueryAuctionItems` is not the same on these builds - Lua cannot ask a C function what arguments it takes, and this repository has never called it once, so both candidate layouts here are hearsay until a client answers. `/family ah query` sends exactly one, gated on `CanSendAuctionQuery`, alternating layouts run by run so two runs cover both, and prints the rows, the total and the first three rows for Alberto to hold against his own auction window - because a layout the client *accepts* can still have queried the wrong thing, and rows arriving is not evidence that it did not. `/family ah` also prints the second return of `GetNumAuctionItems`, never read anywhere before, which is what says how many pages a house is; and whether the newer house has the calls that page past its first five hundred. The one-shot reader is hooked **inside** the scanner's own list-update handler rather than registered beside it, because a second registration under that key would replace the reader the prices come off - L-068 by name. `getAll` is false in both layouts and is not offered as a choice, and the harness holds that rather than a comment. Nothing is said on a mailbox nobody took anything out of. | 2026-09-12 | **What an exchange would put on the wire, weighed without sending it** — backlog 41 | The entry had been open since 2026-09-08 on one figure: the `have` list goes at every exchange, there is an exchange at every login, it is about 6.5 KB counted for two hundred and ten members, and what it *compresses* to was written down as unmeasured with the honest note that a list of similar strings is deflate's best case and expected is not measured. (3) `PayloadMark` answers `entry.mark`; a plain record with none is stamped once. (4) `WarmPayloads` visits only string payloads; `Database:StillCompressed` counts them, and `/family status` prints *storage: plain, N still compressed from an earlier version*, plus a warning line only when the libraries are missing. (6) `Codec:Encode` and `Codec:Preferred` are kept although nothing in the addon calls them: the harness checks the wire's round trip through `Encode`; removing them is not this slice. The world node's tooltip is **two** lines and the second is the profession in the client's own word, which `SkillLines.lua` already resolves to skill 182 or 186 in five locales - so the node's *kind* is an id with nothing new, while *which node* stays a name. The world node also has no frame at all, where every addon pin had a named one. The minimap reading is not evidence: that client runs GatherMate, and nothing in the reading separates its pin from the client's own blip - one more reading with it switched off is wanted. Mists is still unread and has the processor | `docs/DATASOURCES.md`, `docs/BACKLOG.md` 96 |
